TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

12:00:00🚀 LARA MAS v2.2 — Starting multi-agent flow
12:00:00🧭 Supervisor — routing decision
12:00:00State: iter=0 exec_runs=0 explore_runs=0 has_plan=False task_done=False answer=''

12:00:00🔍 Explorer — gpt-4.1-mini (run 1)
12:00:00Pre-injecting API docs for: ['spotify']
📋 Explorer Plan
APP: spotify
REASONING:
- Scope: We need to find the oldest released song across the user's song library, album library, and playlists. This means we must iterate through all songs in the song library, all songs inside each album in the album library, and all songs inside each playlist in the playlist library.
- Metric: The "oldest released song" is determined by the earliest "release_date" field. The "release_date" field is confirmed to exist on songs (from show_song API) and albums (from show_album_library API). Playlists do not have a release_date, so we must get the release_date of each song inside playlists by fetching song details.
- Ambiguities: The task asks for the oldest released song "in my Spotify account from across my song, album and playlist libraries." This means we consider all songs in these three containers, not just the containers themselves. We must get song details to get the release_date for songs in playlists.
- API quirks: 
  - show_song_library returns songs with "song_id" and "title" but no release_date, so we must call show_song for each song to get release_date.
  - show_album_library returns albums with "song_ids" and "release_date" for the album, but the release_date of the album may not be the same as the release_date of each song in it. We must get each song's release_date by calling show_song for each song_id in albums.
  - show_playlist_library returns playlists with "song_ids" but no release_date. We must call show_song for each song_id in playlists to get release_date.
  - The release_date field is in ISO 8601 format (YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS).

PLAN:
1. [spotify] Use login('spotify') to get the access token for Spotify.
2. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_song_library', token) to get ALL songs in the user's song library. [field: 'song_id', 'title']
3. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_album_library', token) to get ALL albums in the user's album library. [field: 'album_id', 'song_ids', 'title']
4. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_playlist_library', token) to get ALL playlists in the user's playlist library. [field: 'playlist_id', 'song_ids', 'title']
5. [spotify] FOR EACH song in song library: Use call_api('spotify', 'show_song', token, song_id=song['song_id']) to get song details including 'release_date'. [field: 'release_date']
6. [spotify] FOR EACH album in album library: FOR EACH song_id in album['song_ids']: Use call_api('spotify', 'show_song', token, song_id=song_id) to get song details including 'release_date'. [field: 'release_date']
7. [spotify] FOR EACH playlist in playlist library: FOR EACH song_id in playlist['song_ids']: Use call_api('spotify', 'show_song', token, song_id=song_id) to get song details including 'release_date'. [field: 'release_date']
8. [generic] Combine all songs collected from steps 5, 6, and 7 into one list. Sort this combined list by 'release_date' ascending (oldest first).
9. [generic] Take the first song from the sorted list and extract its 'title'.
10. [supervisor] apis.supervisor.complete_task(answer=<the_title_of_the_oldest_released_song>)

all_songs = song_details + album_song_details + playlist_song_details

# Remove duplicates by song_id, keep the first occurrence
unique_songs = {}
for song in all_songs:
    sid = song.get('song_id')
    if sid not in unique_songs:
        unique_songs[sid] = song

# Sort songs by release_date ascending (oldest first)
sorted_songs = sorted(unique_songs.values(), key=lambda s: s.get('release_date', '9999-12-31T23:59:59'))

print(f"Total unique songs combined: {len(sorted_songs)}")
print("Oldest song details:", sorted_songs[0])

APP: spotify
REASONING:
- Scope: We need to find the newest released song across the user's song library, album library, and playlists. This means iterating through all songs in the song library, all songs in each album in the album library, and all songs in each playlist in the playlist library.
- Metric: The "newest released" corresponds to the most recent release date. The release date field is 'release_date' confirmed in the show_song and show_album API details.
- Ambiguities: The task asks for the newest released song, so the release date of the song itself is the key metric, not the added_at or created_at dates of libraries or playlists.
- API quirks: 
  - show_song_library returns songs with 'song_id', 'title', and 'added_at' but not 'release_date', so we must call show_song for each song to get 'release_date'.
  - show_album_library returns albums with 'release_date' and 'song_ids', but we need to get each song's details to confirm release_date at the song level.
  - show_playlist_library returns playlists with 'song_ids' and 'created_at' but no release_date, so we must get each song's details for release_date.
  - The release_date field is available in show_song and show_album, but since songs can appear in multiple containers, we must get release_date from show_song for each song to be consistent.

PLAN:
1. [spotify] Use login('spotify') to get the access token for Spotify.
2. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_song_library', token) to get ALL songs in the user's song library. [field: 'song_id', 'title']
3. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_album_library', token) to get ALL albums in the user's album library. [field: 'album_id', 'song_ids']
4. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_playlist_library', token) to get ALL playlists in the user's playlist library. [field: 'playlist_id', 'song_ids']
5. [generic] Combine all song_ids from the song library, all albums' song_ids, and all playlists' song_ids into a single unique set of song_ids.
6. [spotify] FOR EACH unique song_id from step 5: Use call_api('spotify', 'show_song', token, song_id=song_id) to get song details including 'release_date' and 'title'. [field: 'release_date', 'title']
7. [generic] Sort all songs by 'release_date' descending and select the first song as the newest released song.
8. [supervisor] apis.supervisor.complete_task(answer=<title_of_newest_released_song>)

TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

ReAct step 3 / attempt 1
from collections import Counter

artist_counts = Counter()
artist_names = {}

for song in recommended_songs:
    artists = song.get('artists', [])
    for artist in artists:
        artist_id = artist.get('id')
        artist_name = artist.get('name')
        if artist_id is not None:
            artist_counts[artist_id] += 1
            artist_names[artist_id] = artist_name

print("Artist counts:", artist_counts)
least_recommended_artist_id = min(artist_counts, key=artist_counts.get)
least_recommended_artist_name = artist_names[least_recommended_artist_id]
print("Least recommended artist ID:", least_recommended_artist_id)
print("Least recommended artist name:", least_recommended_artist_name)

def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]

    return TOKENS[app]

from datetime import datetime, timedelta

go_to_sleep_alarm = None
other_alarms = []

for alarm in alarms:
    label = alarm.get('label', '').lower()
    if 'go to sleep' in label or 'go-to-sleep' in label:
        go_to_sleep_alarm = alarm
    else:
        other_alarms.append(alarm)

print("Go-to-sleep alarm:", go_to_sleep_alarm)
print("Other alarms count:", len(other_alarms))

# Parse time and add 1 hour
if go_to_sleep_alarm:
    old_time_str = go_to_sleep_alarm['time']
    old_time = datetime.strptime(old_time_str, '%H:%M')
    new_time = (old_time + timedelta(hours=1)).strftime('%H:%M')
    print("Old time:", old_time_str, "New time:", new_time)
else:
    print("No go-to-sleep alarm found.")

ReAct step 1 / attempt 1
TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

    if app in TOKENS:

ReAct step 4 / attempt 1
from datetime import datetime

current_year = datetime.now().year
valid_years = {current_year, current_year - 1}

filtered_songs = []
for song in detailed_songs:
    release_year = int(song.get('release_date', '')[:4]) if song.get('release_date') else None
    genre = song.get('genre', '')
    if release_year in valid_years and 'R&B' in genre:
        filtered_songs.append(song)

ReAct step 1 / attempt 1
TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

from datetime import datetime

current_year = datetime.now().year
filtered_songs = []

for song in detailed_songs:
    genre = song.get('genre', '').lower()
    release_date = song.get('release_date', '')
    release_year = None
    if release_date:
        try:
            release_year = datetime.fromisoformat(release_date).year
        except ValueError:
            pass
    if genre == 'r&b' and release_year == current_year:
        filtered_songs.append(song)

print(f"Filtered songs count: {len(filtered_songs)}")

- Ambiguities: The task asks for the shortest playlist length in minutes. We interpret this as the total duration of all songs in the playlist combined, converted from seconds to minutes and rounded.

6. [generic] Find the minimum playlist duration in minutes among all playlists.

for playlist in detailed_playlists:
    total_duration = 0
    for song in playlist.get('songs', []):

        duration = song_detail.get('duration', 0)
        total_duration += duration
    playlist_durations.append({'playlist_id': playlist['playlist_id'], 'title': playlist['title'], 'duration_seconds': total_duration})

TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

12:00:00🚀 LARA MAS v2.2 — Starting multi-agent flow
12:00:00🧭 Supervisor — routing decision
12:00:00State: iter=0 exec_runs=0 explore_runs=0 has_plan=False task_done=False answer=''

12:00:00🔍 Explorer — gpt-4.1-mini (run 1)
12:00:00Pre-injecting API docs for: ['spotify', 'file_system']
📋 Explorer Plan
APP: spotify
REASONING:
- Scope: We iterate all songs in the user's song library and all albums in the user's album library. For albums, we check all songs in each album to determine if the album is fully downloaded.
- Metric: We use the presence of songs/albums in the liked songs/albums list and the downloaded songs list to decide which to keep. The APIs show_liked_songs, show_liked_albums, show_downloaded_songs, show_song_library, and show_album_library confirm these fields.
- Ambiguities: "An album is downloaded if all songs in it are downloaded" means we must check each song in the album against the downloaded songs list. We keep only albums fully downloaded and liked.
- API quirks: The song and album IDs are respectively 'song_id' and 'album_id'. The downloaded songs API returns songs with 'song_id'. The album details API returns 'songs' with 'id' fields for song IDs.

PLAN:
1. [spotify] Use login('spotify') to get the access token for all subsequent calls.
2. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_song_library', token) to get all songs in the user's song library. [field: 'song_id']
3. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_liked_songs', token) to get all songs the user has liked. [field: 'song_id']
4. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_downloaded_songs', token) to get all songs the user has downloaded. [field: 'song_id']
5. [spotify] For each song in the song library, if the song_id is NOT in both liked songs and downloaded songs, use call_api('spotify', 'remove_song_from_library', token, song_id=song_id) to remove it from the library.
6. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_album_library', token) to get all albums in the user's album library. [field: 'album_id', 'song_ids']
7. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_liked_albums', token) to get all albums the user has liked. [field: 'album_id']
8. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_downloaded_songs', token) again if needed or reuse from step 4 to get all downloaded songs.
9. [spotify] For each album in the album library:
   - Check if album_id is in liked albums.
   - Use call_api('spotify', 'show_album', token, album_id=album_id) to get the list of songs in the album (field 'songs' with 'id').
   - Check if all songs in the album are in the downloaded songs list.
   - If the album is NOT liked or NOT fully downloaded, use call_api('spotify', 'remove_album_from_library', token, album_id=album_id) to remove it from the library.
10. [spotify] apis.supervisor.complete_task(answer=None) to indicate task completion.

TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

        call_api('spotify', 'remove_album_from_library', token, album_id=album_id)
        removed_albums.append(album_id)

ReAct step 5 / attempt 1
# Retrieve the movie recommendations content from the ledger
movie_content = recall_entity('movie recommendations')['content']

# Extract movie titles by splitting lines and filtering out metadata lines
lines = movie_content.split('\n')
movie_titles = []
for line in lines:
    line = line.strip()
    if line and not line.startswith('#') and not line.startswith('-'):
        movie_titles.append(line)

# Join movie titles into a comma-separated string
movie_titles_str = ', '.join(movie_titles)

# Get Christopher's phone number from the contacts found earlier
christopher_phone_number = None
for contact in christopher_contacts:
    if contact.get('first_name') == 'Christopher':
        christopher_phone_number = contact.get('phone_number')
        break

# Send the text message with movie recommendations to Christopher
token_phone = login('phone')
call_api('phone', 'send_text_message', token_phone, phone_number=christopher_phone_number, message=movie_titles_str)

# Complete the task
apis.supervisor.complete_task(answer=None)

ReAct step 1 / attempt 1
TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]
print("Login helper defined.")

Login helper defined.

ReAct step 4 / attempt 1
# Find the note titled "Movie Recommendations"
movie_recs_note = next((n for n in full_notes if n.get('title') == 'Movie Recommendations'), None)
if movie_recs_note:
    content = movie_recs_note.get('content', '')
    # Extract lines that look like movie titles: non-empty lines not starting with '-'
    lines = content.splitlines()
    movie_titles = []
    for line in lines:
        line = line.strip()
        if line and not line.startswith('#') and not line.startswith('-'):
            # Stop if line looks like a detail (e.g. starts with 'director:' or 'genre:')
            if ':' in line and line.split(':')[0].lower() in ['director', 'genre']:
                continue
            movie_titles.append(line)
    # Remove duplicates and join by comma
    movie_titles = list(dict.fromkeys(movie_titles))
    movie_list = ', '.join(movie_titles)
else:
    movie_list = ''
print("Extracted movie list:", movie_list)

TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]
print("Login helper defined.")

from datetime import datetime, timedelta

# Extract the date string from the verbose format
verbose_date = current_datetime['date']  # e.g. 'Thursday, May 18, 2023'
# Parse it to a datetime object
parsed_date = datetime.strptime(verbose_date, '%A, %B %d, %Y')

# Format today's date as YYYY-MM-DD
today_str = parsed_date.strftime('%Y-%m-%d')

# Compute yesterday's date
yesterday = parsed_date - timedelta(days=1)
yesterday_str = yesterday.strftime('%Y-%m-%d')

print("Today's date:", today_str)
print("Yesterday's date:", yesterday_str)

token = login('venmo')
sent_requests = fetch_all_pages('venmo', 'show_sent_payment_requests', token)
print(f"Total sent payment requests: {len(sent_requests)}")

# Filter requests sent to Robert by checking receiver name or email containing 'Robert'
robert_requests = [r for r in sent_requests if 'receiver' in r and r['receiver'] and 
                   (r['receiver'].get('name') and 'Robert' in r['receiver']['name'])]

TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

token = login('venmo')
print("Venmo access token obtained:", token[:10], "...")  # print partial token for verification

📋 Explorer Plan
APP: spotify
REASONING:
- Scope: We need to iterate all songs in the user's Spotify song library to find those that are not liked by the user.
- Metric: The "liked" status is determined by comparing song IDs in the song library and liked songs lists. The rating field is 'rating' confirmed by show_song_reviews API.
- Ambiguities: The task requires rating all songs not liked with 1 star, and if already rated higher, decrease to 1 star. We interpret "already rated it higher" as any existing rating > 1.
- API quirks: show_song_reviews returns reviews with 'song_review_id' as the review ID and includes 'rating' and 'user' with 'email'. review_song creates a review, update_song_review updates an existing review.

PLAN:
1. [spotify] Use login('spotify') to get the access token for Spotify.
2. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_song_library', token) to get ALL songs in the user's song library. [field: 'song_id']
3. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_liked_songs', token) to get ALL songs the user has liked. [field: 'song_id']
4. [generic] Compute the set of song_ids in the library that are NOT in the liked songs set to identify target songs.
5. [spotify] FOR EACH song_id in the target songs: Use call_api('spotify', 'show_song_reviews', token, song_id=song_id, user_email='brenda.webe@gmail.com') to get the user's reviews for that song. [field: 'rating', 'song_review_id']
6. [generic] FOR EACH song review found: If rating > 1, mark for update to rating=1; if no review found, mark for new review with rating=1.
7. [spotify] FOR EACH song marked for update: Use call_api('spotify', 'update_song_review', token, review_id=song_review_id, rating=1) to decrease rating to 1.
8. [spotify] FOR EACH song marked for new review: Use call_api('spotify', 'review_song', token, song_id=song_id, rating=1) to create a new 1-star review.
9. [supervisor] apis.supervisor.complete_task(answer=None)

ReAct step 1 / attempt 1
TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

ReAct step 7 / attempt 1
token = login('spotify')
not_followed_artists = []
for artist_id in reggae_artist_ids:
    following_status = call_api('spotify', 'show_artist_following', token, artist_id=artist_id)
    if not following_status.get('following', False):
        not_followed_artists.append(artist_id)
print(f"Artists not followed yet: {not_followed_artists}")
ReAct step 7 / attempt 1

12:00:00Pre-injecting API docs for: ['spotify']
📋 Explorer Plan
APP: spotify
REASONING:
- Scope: We need to iterate all playlists in the user's playlist library to get all songs in those playlists. Then for each song, we check if its genre is "indie". For all indie songs, we get their artists and follow those artists.
- Metric: The genre of a song is in the 'genre' field of the show_song API response. The artist IDs are in the 'artists' list inside the show_song response, each artist having an 'id' field. We follow artists using the follow_artist API with the artist_id parameter.
- Ambiguities: The task says "indie-genre songs" so we interpret this as the song's genre field exactly matching "indie" (case insensitive). We assume following an artist means calling follow_artist once per artist.
- API quirks: show_playlist_library returns playlists with 'song_ids' list. show_playlist returns detailed playlist info including 'songs' list with 'id' and 'artist_ids'. show_song returns 'genre' and 'artists' list with 'id' and 'name'. follow_artist requires artist_id and access_token.

PLAN:
1. [spotify] Use login('spotify') to get the access token for Spotify.

def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:

    if app in TOKENS:

ReAct step 7 / attempt 1
import re

def parse_shares(note_content):
    shares = {}
    lines = note_content.splitlines()
    for line in lines:
        match = re.match(r'-\s*(.+?)\s*=>\s*\$(\d+)', line)
        if match:
            name = match.group(1).strip()
            amount = int(match.group(2))
            shares[name] = amount
    return shares

shares_3084 = parse_shares(note1.get('content', ''))
shares_3085 = parse_shares(note2.get('content', ''))

# Remember entities with amounts from both notes
for name, amount in {**shares_3084, **shares_3085}.items():
    remember_entity(name, amount=amount)

print("Parsed shares from notes:", {**shares_3084, **shares_3085})

    email = c.get('email')

# Build a mapping from lowercase first name to email (if unique)
first_name_to_email = {}
for c in contacts:
    first_name = c.get('first_name', '').lower()
    email = c.get('email')
    if first_name and email:
        if first_name in first_name_to_email:
            # Mark as ambiguous if multiple emails for same first name
            first_name_to_email[first_name] = None
        else:
            first_name_to_email[first_name] = email

# Map entities by first name to email if unique
for entity in all_entities():
    name = entity['name']
    first_name = name.split()[0].lower()
    email = first_name_to_email.get(first_name)
    if email:
        remember_entity(name, email=email)
    else:
        print(f"Warning: No unique email found for first name {first_name} of {name}")

# Print entities with emails now
entities_with_emails = [e for e in all_entities() if e.get('email')]

from datetime import datetime, timedelta

# Get today's date from phone app to compute yesterday's date
current_datetime = call_api('phone', 'get_current_date_and_time', token_phone)
today_str = current_datetime.get('date', '')  # Expecting 'YYYY-MM-DD'
if not today_str:
    today_str = datetime.utcnow().strftime('%Y-%m-%d')
today_date = datetime.strptime(today_str, '%Y-%m-%d')
yesterday_date = today_date - timedelta(days=1)
yesterday_str = yesterday_date.strftime('%Y-%m-%d')

transactions_yesterday = fetch_all_pages('venmo', 'show_transactions', token_venmo,
                                         direction='received',
                                         min_created_at=yesterday_str,
                                         max_created_at=yesterday_str)
print(f"Fetched {len(transactions_yesterday)} transactions received yesterday ({yesterday_str}).")

# Extract emails of senders who paid yesterday
paid_emails = set()
for tx in transactions_yesterday:
    sender_email = tx.get('sender', {}).get('email')
    if sender_email:
        paid_emails.add(sender_email)

print("Emails of people who paid yesterday:", paid_emails)

from datetime import datetime, timedelta

current_datetime = call_api('phone', 'get_current_date_and_time', token_phone)
today_str = current_datetime.get('date', '')  # e.g. 'Saturday, May 20, 2023'

# Parse date string like 'Saturday, May 20, 2023'
try:
    today_date = datetime.strptime(today_str, '%A, %B %d, %Y')
except Exception as e:
    print("Date parsing error:", e)
    today_date = datetime.utcnow()

yesterday_date = today_date - timedelta(days=1)
yesterday_str = yesterday_date.strftime('%Y-%m-%d')

transactions_yesterday = fetch_all_pages('venmo', 'show_transactions', token_venmo,
                                         direction='received',
                                         min_created_at=yesterday_str,
                                         max_created_at=yesterday_str)
print(f"Fetched {len(transactions_yesterday)} transactions received yesterday ({yesterday_str}).")

paid_emails = set()
for tx in transactions_yesterday:
    sender_email = tx.get('sender', {}).get('email')
    if sender_email:
        paid_emails.add(sender_email)

print("Emails of people who paid yesterday:", paid_emails)

for entity in all_entities():
    name = entity['name']
    email = entity.get('email')
    amount = entity.get('amount')
    if email and amount:
        if email not in paid_emails and email not in pending_emails:
            print(f"Creating payment request for {name} ({email}) amount ${amount}")

12:21:11Pre-injecting API docs for: ['venmo', 'simple_note', 'phone']


📋 Explorer Plan
APP: simple_note, venmo, phone
REASONING:
  - Scope: We need to iterate all notes in simple_note to find the note containing individual shares for the dinner. We then need to get all Venmo transactions from yesterday to identify who has already paid. We will also need to find the contacts of friends from the phone app to get their emails for payment requests.
  - Metric: For simple_note, we use search_notes to find notes and show_note to get note content. For venmo, show_transactions lists transactions with fields including 'sender' and 'receiver' emails and 'created_at' date. For phone, search_contacts returns contacts with emails.
  - Ambiguities: The note content format is unknown, so we will parse it to extract friend names and amounts. "Friends Dinner" is the description for payment requests. "Yesterday" is relative to the current date from phone's get_current_date_and_time.
  - API quirks: simple_note search_notes does not return note content, so show_note is needed for content. Venmo transactions have 'created_at' in YYYY-MM-DD format for filtering. Phone search_contacts returns emails needed for Venmo payment requests.

PLAN:
1. [simple_note] Use login('simple_note') to get the access token for simple_note.
2. [venmo] Use login('venmo') to get the access token for venmo.
3. [phone] Use login('phone') to get the access token for phone.
4. [phone] Use call_api('phone', 'get_current_date_and_time', phone_token) to get today's date. Compute yesterday's date string for filtering transactions.
5. [simple_note] Use fetch_all_pages('simple_note', 'search_notes', simple_note_token, query='dinner') to find notes related to the dinner. Identify the note_id of the note with individual shares.
6. [simple_note] Use call_api('simple_note', 'show_note', simple_note_token, note_id=<note_id>) to get the content of the note with individual shares.
7. [generic] Parse the note content to extract a list of friends with their individual share amounts.
8. [venmo] Use fetch_all_pages('venmo', 'show_transactions', venmo_token, min_created_at=<yesterday>, max_created_at=<yesterday>, direction='received') to get transactions where friends have sent money to you yesterday.
9. [generic] Extract the emails of friends who have already paid from the transactions.
10. [phone] For each friend in the note who has not paid, use fetch_all_pages('phone', 'search_contacts', phone_token, query=<friend_name>) to find their email addresses.
11. [venmo] For each friend who has not paid and whose email was found, use call_api('venmo', 'create_payment_request', venmo_token, user_email=<friend_email>, amount=<share_amount>, description='Friends Dinner', private=False) to send a payment request.

ReAct step 1 / attempt 1
TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]
print("Login helper defined.")

ReAct step 9 / attempt 1
import re

content = note.get('content')
lines = content.splitlines()
shares = {}
for line in lines:
    match = re.match(r'- (\w+) => \$([0-9]+)', line.strip())
    if match:
        name = match.group(1)
        amount = int(match.group(2))
        shares[name] = amount
        remember_entity(name, amount=amount)

print("Parsed shares:")
print(shares)

paid_emails = {t['sender']['email'] for t in transactions}

# Map full names from transactions to first names in ledger for matching
paid_names = set()
for t in transactions:
    full_name = t['sender']['name']
    first_name = full_name.split()[0]
    paid_names.add(first_name)

print("First names of friends who have paid:", paid_names)

# Find friends who have not paid yet
all_names = set(shares.keys())
not_paid_names = all_names - paid_names
print("Friends who have not paid yet:", not_paid_names)

ReAct step 12 / attempt 1
for name in not_paid_names:
    contacts = fetch_all_pages('phone', 'search_contacts', phone_token, query=name)
    email = None
    for c in contacts:
        # Match by first name only, case insensitive
        if c.get('first_name', '').lower() == name.lower():
            email = c.get('email')
            if email:
                remember_entity(name, email=email)
                break
    if email:
        amount = recall_entity(name).get('amount')
        print(f"Sending payment request to {name} ({email}) for ${amount}")
        call_api('venmo', 'create_payment_request', venmo_token,
                 user_email=email, amount=amount, description='Friends Dinner', private=False)
    else:
        print(f"No email found for {name}, skipping payment request.")

  - Metric: Use simple_note's search_notes and show_note to get note content. Use venmo's show_transactions with min_created_at and max_created_at to filter transactions by date. Use venmo's create_payment_request to send payment requests. Use phone's get_current_date_and_time to get today's date and compute yesterday's date.
  - Ambiguities: The note content format is not specified, so assume it contains names/emails and amounts. The task says "some coworkers" but does not specify who; we rely on the note for the full list. Payment requests are sent only to those who have not paid yet.
  - API quirks: simple_note search_notes does not return content, so must call show_note for content. venmo show_transactions supports filtering by date with min_created_at and max_created_at in YYYY-MM-DD format. venmo create_payment_request requires user_email, amount, description, and optional privacy flag.

PLAN:
1. [simple_note] Use login('simple_note') to get access token. Use fetch_all_pages('simple_note', 'search_notes', token) to get all notes and find the note related to the dinner shares by searching for keywords like "dinner" or "colleagues" in the title or tags. [field: 'title', 'tags']
2. [simple_note] Use call_api('simple_note', 'show_note', token, note_id=<id_of_found_note>) to get the content of the note with individual shares. Parse the content to extract a list of coworkers with their names/emails and amounts owed. [field: 'content']
3. [phone] Use login('phone') to get access token. Use call_api('phone', 'get_current_date_and_time', token) to get today's date. Compute yesterday's date in YYYY-MM-DD format. [field: 'date']
4. [venmo] Use login('venmo') to get access token. Use fetch_all_pages('venmo', 'show_transactions', token, min_created_at=<yesterday>, max_created_at=<yesterday>, direction='received') to get all transactions where you received money yesterday. Extract the emails of coworkers who have already paid. [field: 'created_at', 'receiver.email']
5. [generic] Compare the list of coworkers from the note with the list of those who already paid on Venmo. Identify coworkers who have not paid yet and their owed amounts.
6. [venmo] FOR EACH coworker who has not paid yet: Use call_api('venmo', 'create_payment_request', token, user_email=<coworker_email>, amount=<owed_amount>, description="Dinner with Colleagues", private=False) to send a payment request with the description note. [field: 'user_email', 'amount', 'description']

ReAct step 1 / attempt 1
TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

token = login('simple_note')
notes = fetch_all_pages('simple_note', 'search_notes', token)
print(notes)

ReAct step 3 / attempt 1
import re

content = note['content']
lines = content.split('\n')
for line in lines:
    match = re.match(r'- (.+) => \$([0-9]+)', line.strip())
    if match:
        name = match.group(1)
        amount = int(match.group(2))
        remember_entity(name, amount=amount)

print(all_entities())

ReAct step 8 / attempt 1
# Map transaction senders' first names to emails
paid_senders = {}
for tx in transactions:
    sender_name = tx.get('sender', {}).get('name', '')
    sender_email = tx.get('sender', {}).get('email', '').lower()
    if sender_name and sender_email:
        first_name = sender_name.split()[0].lower()
        paid_senders[first_name] = sender_email

print("Paid senders mapping:", paid_senders)

# Update ledger entities with emails if paid
for entity in all_entities():
    name_lower = entity['name'].lower()
    if name_lower in paid_senders:
        remember_entity(entity['name'], email=paid_senders[name_lower])

print("Updated entities with emails where paid:")
print(all_entities())

for entity in all_entities():
    if 'email' not in entity:
        # Lookup email by name in phone contacts
        contacts = fetch_all_pages('phone', 'search_contacts', token_phone, query=entity['name'])
        if contacts:
            email = contacts[0].get('email')
            if email:
                remember_entity(entity['name'], email=email)
                amount = entity['amount']
                # Create payment request
                response = call_api('venmo', 'create_payment_request', token_venmo,
                                    user_email=email,
                                    amount=amount,
                                    description="Dinner with Colleagues",
                                    private=False)
                print(f"Payment request sent to {entity['name']} ({email}):", response)
            else:
                print(f"No email found for {entity['name']} in contacts.")
        else:
            print(f"No contacts found for {entity['name']}.")

TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

ReAct step 4 / attempt 1
from datetime import datetime

categorized_files = {'Athens': [], 'Seoul': [], 'Paris': []}
for f in file_details:
    created_at = f.get('created_at')
    if created_at:
        dt = datetime.fromisoformat(created_at)
        if dt.month == 1:
            categorized_files['Athens'].append(f['path'])
        elif dt.month == 4:
            categorized_files['Seoul'].append(f['path'])
        else:
            categorized_files['Paris'].append(f['path'])
    else:
        # If no creation date, default to Paris
        categorized_files['Paris'].append(f['path'])

print(categorized_files)

token = login('file_system')
base_dir = '/home/susan/photographs/vacations'

for vacation, paths in categorized_files.items():
    for src_path in paths:
        filename = src_path.split('/')[-1]
        dest_path = f"{base_dir}/{vacation}/{filename}"
        call_api('file_system', 'move_file', token,
                 source_file_path=src_path,
                 destination_file_path=dest_path,
                 overwrite=False,
                 retain_dates=True)
        print(f"Moved {src_path} to {dest_path}")

ReAct step 1 / attempt 1
TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]
print("Login helper defined.")

ReAct step 9 / attempt 1
import re

content = """
Electricity Bill from ElectraSource for duration => 2023-04-01 - 2023-04-30

Jose Harrison
172 Matthew Knolls Suite 730
Seattle
Washington
United States
65644
Account Number => 927512796
Meter Reading (kWh) => 200 kWh
Electricity Charges => $99
Service Fee => $10.00
Taxes and Fees => $10.0
Total Amount => $119
"""

# Extract total amount using regex
match = re.search(r'Total Amount\s*=>\s*\$(\d+(\.\d+)?)', content)
bill_amount = float(match.group(1)) if match else 0.0
print("Extracted bill amount:", bill_amount)

# Calculate amount per person (Jose + roommates)
roommates = [e for e in all_entities()]
num_people = len(roommates) + 1  # including Jose
amount_per_person = round(bill_amount / num_people, 2)
print("Amount per person:", amount_per_person)

venmo_token = login('venmo')
for roommate in roommates:
    email = roommate.get('email')
    if email:
        response = call_api('venmo', 'create_payment_request', venmo_token, user_email=email, amount=amount_per_person, description='For electricity bill.', private=False)
        print(f"Payment request sent to {email}: {response}")
        remember_entity(roommate.get('name', ''), payment_request_id=response.get('payment_request_id'))
apis.supervisor.complete_task(answer=None)

ReAct step 1 / attempt 1
TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

import re

# Filter cable bill files
cable_files = [f for f in files if f.startswith('/home/jennifer/bills/cable/') and f.endswith('.txt')]
print("Cable bill files:", cable_files)

# Extract dates from filenames and sort descending
def extract_date(filename):
    match = re.search(r'(\d{4}-\d{2})\.txt$', filename)
    return match.group(1) if match else ''

cable_files_sorted = sorted(cable_files, key=extract_date, reverse=True)
print("Sorted cable bill files:", cable_files_sorted)

# Pick the most recent file
if cable_files_sorted:
    latest_cable_file = cable_files_sorted[0]
    receipt_content = call_api('file_system', 'show_file', token_fs, file_path=latest_cable_file).get('content', '')
    print("Latest cable bill file:", latest_cable_file)
    print("Receipt content:", receipt_content)
else:
    print("No cable bill files found.")

📋 Explorer Plan
APP: spotify, file_system
REASONING:
  - Scope: We need to iterate all songs in the user's song library, all albums in the user's album library (and their songs), and all playlists in the user's playlist library (and their songs) to get a unique list of all songs.
  - Metric: The song title is in the 'title' field and artists are in the 'artists' list with each artist having a 'name' field. These fields are confirmed by the show_song_library, show_album_library, show_playlist_library, and show_playlist APIs.
  - Ambiguities: The task requires a unique list of songs across all sources. Songs may appear in multiple places, so deduplication by song_id is necessary. Artists should be concatenated with "|" separator.
  - API quirks: 
    - show_playlist returns songs with 'id' and 'artist_ids', but not artist names directly. We will need to get artist names from the song details (show_song).
    - show_album_library returns 'song_ids' but not song details, so we must fetch each song detail separately.
    - show_song_library and show_playlist_library provide song details including artists.
    - The file_system create_file API requires the file path, access token, content, and an overwrite flag.

PLAN:
1. [spotify] Use login('spotify') to get the access token for Spotify.
2. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_song_library', token) to get all songs in the user's song library. [field: 'song_id', 'title', 'artists']
3. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_album_library', token) to get all albums in the user's album library. [field: 'album_id', 'song_ids']
4. [spotify] For each album from step 3, use call_api('spotify', 'show_album', token, album_id=album['album_id']) to get detailed album info including songs with 'id' and 'title' and artist ids. [field: 'songs' with 'id', 'title', 'artist_ids']
5. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_playlist_library', token) to get all playlists in the user's playlist library. [field: 'playlist_id', 'song_ids']
6. [spotify] For each playlist from step 5, use call_api('spotify', 'show_playlist', token, playlist_id=playlist['playlist_id']) to get detailed playlist info including songs with 'id', 'title', and 'artist_ids'. [field: 'songs' with 'id', 'title', 'artist_ids']
7. [spotify] Combine all songs from steps 2, 4, and 6 into a single collection, deduplicate by song_id.
8. [spotify] For any songs from albums and playlists where artist names are not directly available, use call_api('spotify', 'show_song', token, song_id=song_id) to get full song details including artist names.
9. [generic] Construct CSV content with headers "Title,Artists" and each song's title and artists joined by "|" as the artist separator.
10. [file_system] Use login('file_system') to get the access token for the file system.
11. [file_system] Use call_api('file_system', 'create_file', token, file_path="~/backups/spotify.csv", content=<CSV content>, overwrite=True) to create the CSV backup file.
12. [spotify] Use call_api('spotify', 'delete_account', token) to terminate the Spotify account.

ReAct step 1 / attempt 1
TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]
print("Login helper defined.")

Login helper defined.

📋 Explorer Plan
APP: spotify, file_system
REASONING:
  - Scope: We need to iterate all songs in the user's song library, all albums in the user's album library (and their songs), and all playlists in the user's playlist library (and their songs). This ensures we get a comprehensive unique list of all songs across these containers.
  - Metric: The song title is in the 'title' field and artists are in the 'artists' list with each artist having a 'name' field. These fields are confirmed by the show_song_library, show_album_library, show_playlist_library, show_playlist, and show_song APIs.
  - Ambiguities: The task requires a unique list of songs, so duplicates across libraries and playlists must be removed. Artists should be concatenated with "|" as separator.
  - API quirks: 
    * show_playlist returns songs with 'id', 'title', and 'artist_ids' but not full artist names, so we will rely on song details from show_song to get artist names.
    * show_album_library returns albums with 'song_ids' but not full song details, so we will call show_song for each song_id to get title and artists.
    * show_song_library returns songs with full details including 'title' and 'artists'.
    * show_playlist_library returns playlists with 'song_ids', but we need to call show_playlist for each playlist to get song details.
    * The file_system create_file API requires 'file_path', 'access_token', 'content', and optional 'overwrite' flag.
    * The spotify delete_account API requires only the access_token.

PLAN:
1. [spotify] Use login('spotify') to get the Spotify access token for all subsequent Spotify API calls.
2. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_song_library', token) to get all songs in the user's song library. [field: 'title', 'artists']
3. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_album_library', token) to get all albums in the user's album library. [field: 'album_id', 'song_ids']
4. [spotify] FOR EACH album in album library: Use call_api('spotify', 'show_album', token, album_id=album['album_id']) to get detailed album info including songs. [field: 'songs' with 'id', 'title', 'artist_ids']
5. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_playlist_library', token) to get all playlists in the user's playlist library. [field: 'playlist_id']
6. [spotify] FOR EACH playlist in playlist library: Use call_api('spotify', 'show_playlist', token, playlist_id=playlist['playlist_id']) to get detailed playlist info including songs. [field: 'songs' with 'id', 'title', 'artist_ids']
7. [spotify] Aggregate all unique song IDs from steps 2, 4, and 6. For each unique song ID not already fully detailed, use call_api('spotify', 'show_song', token, song_id=song_id) to get full song details including 'title' and 'artists'.
8. [file_system] Use login('file_system') to get the File System access token.
9. [generic] Construct CSV content with headers "Title,Artists". For each unique song, write a line with the song's title and the artists' names joined by "|".
10. [file_system] Use call_api('file_system', 'create_file', token, file_path="~/backups/spotify_library.csv", content=<CSV content>, overwrite=True) to create or overwrite the CSV backup file.
11. [spotify] Use call_api('spotify', 'delete_account', token) to terminate the Spotify account after backup is complete.
12. [supervisor] apis.supervisor.complete_task(answer=None)

ReAct step 1 / attempt 1
TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

ReAct step 7 / attempt 1
token = login('spotify')

# Collect song details from song library (already detailed)
song_library_songs = {s['song_id']: s for s in songs}

# Collect song IDs from album songs and playlist songs
album_song_ids = {s['id'] for s in album_songs}
playlist_song_ids = {s['id'] for s in playlist_songs}

# Combine all song IDs
all_song_ids = set(song_library_songs.keys()) | album_song_ids | playlist_song_ids

# Identify which song IDs need full details (not in song_library_songs)
missing_song_ids = all_song_ids - set(song_library_songs.keys())

# Fetch full details for missing songs
for sid in missing_song_ids:
    full_song = call_api('spotify', 'show_song', token, song_id=sid)
    # Normalize to match song_library_songs structure
    song_library_songs[sid] = {
        'song_id': sid,
        'title': full_song.get('title'),
        'artists': full_song.get('artists', [])
    }

print(f"Total unique songs collected: {len(song_library_songs)}")
print("Sample detailed song entry:", next(iter(song_library_songs.values())))

lines = ["Title,Artists"]
for song in song_library_songs.values():
    title = song.get('title', '').replace('\n', ' ').replace('\r', ' ').replace('"', '""')

12:00:00🔍 Explorer — gpt-4.1-mini (run 1)


12:00:00Pre-injecting API docs for: ['spotify', 'file_system']


📋 Explorer Plan
APP: spotify, file_system
REASONING:
  - Scope: We need to iterate all songs in the user's song library, all albums in the user's album library (and their songs), and all playlists in the user's playlist library (and their songs). This ensures we get a unique list of all songs across these containers.
  - Metric: The song title is 'title' and the artists are in a list under 'artists' with each artist having a 'name' field. This is confirmed by the show_song_library, show_album_library, show_playlist, and show_song APIs in Spotify.
  - Ambiguities: The task requires a unique list of songs by title and artists. We will consider uniqueness by song_id to avoid duplicates. Artists should be concatenated with "|" separator.
  - API quirks: 
    - show_playlist returns songs with 'id' and 'artist_ids', so we need to fetch song details to get artist names.
    - show_album returns songs with 'id' and 'artist_ids' as well, so we also need to fetch song details for artist names.
    - show_song_library returns songs with full artist info, so no extra calls needed there.
    - We must login to both spotify and file_system apps.
    - File creation requires file_path, access_token, content, and optional overwrite flag.

PLAN:
1. [spotify] Use login('spotify') to get the Spotify access token for all subsequent Spotify API calls.
2. [file_system] Use login('file_system') to get the File System access token for file operations.
3. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_song_library', spotify_token) to get all songs in the user's song library. [field: 'song_id', 'title', 'artists']
4. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_album_library', spotify_token) to get all albums in the user's album library. [field: 'album_id', 'song_ids']
5. [spotify] For each album from step 4, use call_api('spotify', 'show_album', spotify_token, album_id=album['album_id']) to get detailed album info including songs with 'id' (song_id). Collect all song_ids from all albums. [field: 'songs' with 'id']
6. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_playlist_library', spotify_token) to get all playlists in the user's playlist library. [field: 'playlist_id', 'song_ids']
7. [spotify] For each playlist from step 6, use call_api('spotify', 'show_playlist', spotify_token, playlist_id=playlist['playlist_id']) to get detailed playlist info including songs with 'id' (song_id). Collect all song_ids from all playlists. [field: 'songs' with 'id']
8. [generic] Combine all song_ids from steps 3 (song library), 5 (albums), and 7 (playlists) into a unique set of song_ids.
9. [spotify] For each unique song_id from step 8, use call_api('spotify', 'show_song', spotify_token, song_id=song_id) to get song details including 'title' and 'artists' (list of artist objects with 'name').
10. [generic] Create CSV content string with header "Title,Artists\n". For each song from step 9, append a line with the song's title and the artists' names joined by "|" (pipe character).
11. [file_system] Use call_api('file_system', 'create_file', file_system_token, file_path="~/backups/spotify_songs.csv", content=<CSV content from step 10>, overwrite=True) to create or overwrite the CSV backup file.
12. [spotify] Use call_api('spotify', 'delete_account', spotify_token) to terminate the Spotify account after backup is complete.
13. [supervisor] apis.supervisor.complete_task(answer=None)

ReAct step 1 / attempt 1
TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]
print("Login helper defined.")

csv_lines = ["Title,Artists"]
for song in songs_details:
    title = song.get('title', '').replace('"', '""')
    artists = song.get('artists', [])

    csv_lines.append(f'"{title}","{artist_names}"')

23:55:00Pre-injecting API docs for: ['venmo', 'phone']


📋 Explorer Plan
APP: venmo, phone
REASONING:
  - Scope: We need to iterate all transactions received by the user in the last 7 days (including today) from friends. We will filter transactions by direction='received' and by created_at date range. We will also identify friends by their email addresses to filter transactions from friends only.
  - Metric: The transaction's 'created_at' field is used to filter by date range, confirmed by the venmo show_transactions API. The 'sender' field in transactions contains the sender's email to identify friends. The 'transaction_id' field is used to comment and like transactions.
  - Ambiguities: The task specifies "payments I received from my friends," so we must filter transactions by direction='received' and sender being a friend. The last 7 days includes today, so we will get the current date from phone app and compute the date 7 days ago.
  - API quirks: show_transactions supports filtering by direction, min_created_at, max_created_at. The date format is YYYY-MM-DD. The like_transaction and create_transaction_comment APIs require transaction_id and access_token. There is no direct API to get friends list, so we will consider all senders in received transactions as friends (since the task says "from my friends," we assume all senders in received transactions are friends).

PLAN:
1. [venmo] Use login('venmo') to get the access token for Venmo API calls.
2. [phone] Use login('phone') and call_api('phone', 'get_current_date_and_time', phone_token) to get the current date. Compute the date 7 days ago in YYYY-MM-DD format.  [field: 'date']
3. [venmo] Use fetch_all_pages('venmo', 'show_transactions', token, direction='received', min_created_at=<7_days_ago_date>, max_created_at=<current_date>) to get all transactions received in the last 7 days.  [field: 'transaction_id', 'created_at', 'sender.email']
4. [generic] Filter the transactions from step 3 to only those where the sender is a friend (all senders in received transactions are considered friends as per task).
5. [venmo] FOR EACH filtered transaction from step 4: call call_api('venmo', 'create_transaction_comment', token, transaction_id=<transaction_id>, comment="Thanks!") to add the comment "Thanks!" to the transaction.  [field: 'transaction_id']
6. [venmo] FOR EACH filtered transaction from step 4: call call_api('venmo', 'like_transaction', token, transaction_id=<transaction_id>) to like the transaction.  [field: 'transaction_id']
7. [supervisor] apis.supervisor.complete_task(answer=None)

phone_token = login('phone')
current_datetime_str = call_api('phone', 'get_current_date_and_time', phone_token).get('date')

# Parse current date and compute 7 days ago date
current_date = datetime.strptime(current_datetime_str[:10], '%Y-%m-%d')
seven_days_ago_date = current_date - timedelta(days=6)  # last 7 days including today means 6 days before today
seven_days_ago_str = seven_days_ago_date.strftime('%Y-%m-%d')

        remember_entity(sender.get('name', 'Unknown'), email=sender.get('email'))

    if transaction_id:

                            transaction_id=transaction_id, comment="Thanks!")

TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]
print("Login helper defined.")

ReAct step 7 / attempt 1
# Determine the needed number of songs from the workout plan content
import re

content = workout_note_full.get('content', '')
# Extract all duration_mins values
durations = [int(x) for x in re.findall(r'duration_mins:\s*(\d+)', content)]
print("Workout durations (minutes) found:", durations)

# Use the maximum duration as a proxy for needed songs (assuming 1 song per minute)
needed_songs = max(durations) if durations else 0
print("Needed number of songs for workout:", needed_songs)

# Find playlist with the smallest number of songs >= needed_songs
valid_playlists = [p for p in playlist_song_counts if p[1] >= needed_songs]
if not valid_playlists:
    # No playlist meets the needed songs, pick the one with the most songs
    chosen_playlist = max(playlist_song_counts, key=lambda x: x[1])
else:
    # Pick the playlist with the smallest number of songs that meets or exceeds needed_songs
    chosen_playlist = min(valid_playlists, key=lambda x: x[1])

print("Chosen playlist (playlist_id, song_count, title):", chosen_playlist)

    - Spotify's show_playlist_library returns playlists with 'playlist_id' and 'song_ids' (list of song IDs).

    - Simple Note's search_notes can be used to find the workout plan note by searching for keywords like "workout".
    - Simple Note's show_note returns the note content which we will parse to determine workout length.

ReAct step 1 / attempt 1
TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

import datetime
token_sn = login('simple_note')
note_content = call_api('simple_note', 'show_note', token_sn, note_id=681)['content']

# Determine today's day name in lowercase
today_day = datetime.datetime.now().strftime('%A').lower()

# Parse the note content to find the duration for today's workout
duration_mins = None
for block in note_content.split('\n\n'):
    if block.startswith(f'day: {today_day}'):
        for line in block.split('\n'):
            if line.startswith('duration_mins:'):
                duration_mins = int(line.split(':')[1].strip())
                break
        if duration_mins is not None:
            break

print(f"Today's workout duration (mins): {duration_mins}")

# Now login to Spotify and fetch playlists
token_spotify = login('spotify')
playlists = fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_playlist_library', token_spotify)
print(f"Found {len(playlists)} playlists in Spotify library")
print(playlists[:3])  # print first 3 playlists metadata for inspection

ReAct step 5 / attempt 1
token_spotify = login('spotify')
playlists = fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_playlist_library', token_spotify)

detailed_playlists = []
for pl in playlists:
    pl_detail = call_api('spotify', 'show_playlist', token_spotify, playlist_id=pl['playlist_id'])
    song_count = len(pl_detail.get('songs', []))
    detailed_playlists.append({'playlist_id': pl['playlist_id'], 'title': pl.get('title'), 'song_count': song_count})

print(detailed_playlists)

# Find playlist with song_count >= 90 (assuming 1 song ~ 1 min for simplicity)
suitable_playlists = [pl for pl in detailed_playlists if pl['song_count'] >= 90]
print("Suitable playlists:", suitable_playlists)

ReAct step 6 / attempt 1
token_spotify = login('spotify')
playlists = fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_playlist_library', token_spotify)

detailed_playlists = []
for pl in playlists:
    pl_detail = call_api('spotify', 'show_playlist', token_spotify, playlist_id=pl['playlist_id'])
    song_count = len(pl_detail.get('songs', []))
    detailed_playlists.append({'playlist_id': pl['playlist_id'], 'title': pl.get('title'), 'song_count': song_count})

# Sort playlists by song_count descending to find the longest playlist available
sorted_playlists = sorted(detailed_playlists, key=lambda x: x['song_count'], reverse=True)
print("Playlists sorted by song count:", sorted_playlists)

# Select the playlist with the most songs, even if less than workout duration
if sorted_playlists and sorted_playlists[0]['song_count'] > 0:
    selected_playlist = sorted_playlists[0]
else:
    selected_playlist = None

print("Selected playlist:", selected_playlist)

ReAct step 1 / attempt 1
TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

import re

token_spotify = login('spotify')

content = workout_note['content']
# Extract all duration_mins values from the note content

ReAct step 5 / attempt 1
token_spotify = login('spotify')

playlist_durations = []
for pl in playlists:
    pl_detail = call_api('spotify', 'show_playlist', token_spotify, playlist_id=pl['playlist_id'])
    songs = pl_detail.get('songs', [])
    total_duration = sum(song.get('duration', 0) for song in songs) / 60  # convert seconds to minutes
    playlist_durations.append({
        'playlist_id': pl['playlist_id'],
        'title': pl['title'],
        'total_duration_mins': total_duration,
        'song_count': len(songs)
    })

print(playlist_durations)

ReAct step 6 / attempt 1
token_spotify = login('spotify')

playlist_durations = []
for pl in playlists:
    pl_detail = call_api('spotify', 'show_playlist', token_spotify, playlist_id=pl['playlist_id'])
    songs = pl_detail.get('songs', [])
    total_duration = 0
    for song in songs:
        song_detail = call_api('spotify', 'show_song', song['id'])
        total_duration += song_detail.get('duration', 0)
    total_duration_mins = total_duration / 60  # convert seconds to minutes
    playlist_durations.append({
        'playlist_id': pl['playlist_id'],
        'title': pl['title'],
        'total_duration_mins': total_duration_mins,
        'song_count': len(songs)
    })

print(playlist_durations)
